# Proposed OEIS comments: A207302 to A208318

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A207302

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A207302>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [2799](../papers/2501-3000/2799-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = (8/3)*n^4 + (49/3)*n^3 + (16/3)*n^2 - (43/3)*n + 3.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jun 21 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical closed form is correct. The count is a walk count in a finite graph, so it satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence; the conjectured form satisfies the same one, and the two agree at enough consecutive indices to agree from then on, for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A207306

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A207306>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [8207](../papers/8001-8500/8207-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = a(n-1) + a(n-2) + 6*a(n-3) + 4*a(n-4) + a(n-5) - 2*a(n-6) - 2*a(n-7) for n>9.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(6 + 30*x + 56*x^2 + 101*x^3 + 56*x^4 + x^5 - 38*x^6 - 26*x^7 - 2*x^8) / (1 - x - x^2 - 6*x^3 - 4*x^4 - x^5 + 2*x^6 + 2*x^7). - _Colin Barker_, Mar 05 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 36 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 9. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

> Empirical: a(n) = (1/36)*n^7 + (4/9)*n^6 + (53/18)*n^5 + (91/9)*n^4 + (589/36)*n^3 + (31/9)*n^2 - (58/3)*n + 6.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jun 22 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical closed form is correct. The count is a walk count in a finite graph, so it satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence; the conjectured form satisfies the same one, and the two agree at enough consecutive indices to agree from then on, for n > 8. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A207449

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A207449>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3037](../papers/3001-3500/3037-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 10*n^3 + 10*n^2 - 10*n.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jun 22 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 111 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A207596

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A207596>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3013](../papers/3001-3500/3013-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 30*n^3 + 15*n^2 - 45*n + 15.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jun 25 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 241 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 36 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 8. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A208046>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [8849](../papers/8501-9000/8849-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 12*a(n-1) - 40*a(n-2) + 68*a(n-3) - 64*a(n-4) for n>5.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(5 - 28*x + 60*x^2 - 84*x^3 + 32*x^4) / (1 - 12*x + 40*x^2 - 68*x^3 + 64*x^4). - _Colin Barker_, Mar 06 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 4 018 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A208288

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A208288>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [8676](../papers/8501-9000/8676-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) + 6*a(n-2) - 4*a(n-3) - 2*a(n-4) + a(n-5).
> Empirical g.f.: 8*x*(1 + 5*x - 3*x^2 - 2*x^3 + x^4) / ((1 + x - x^2)*(1 - 4*x - x^2 + x^3)). - _Colin Barker_, Jun 30 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 64 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
